Another difficulty here is that spin foams are defined on a discretization of spacetime. While this presents no problems for a topological field theory as it has no local degrees of freedom, it presents problems for GR. This is known as the problem triangularization dependence. 
### Modern formulation of spin foams
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=15 "Edit section: Modern formulation of spin foams")]
Just as imposing the classical simplicity constraint recovers general relativity from BF theory, it is expected that an appropriate quantum simplicity constraint will recover quantum gravity from quantum BF theory. 
Progress has been made with regard to this issue by Engle, Pereira, and Rovelli,[[20]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEnglePereiraRovelli2009161301-20) Freidel and Krasnov[[21]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreidelKrasnov2008125018-21) and Livine and Speziale[[22]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTELivineSpeziale200850004-22) in defining spin foam interaction amplitudes with better behaviour. 
An attempt to make contact between EPRL-FK spin foam and the canonical formulation of LQG has been made.[[23]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlesciThiemannZipfel2011024017-23)
### Spin foam derived from the master constraint operator
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=16 "Edit section: Spin foam derived from the master constraint operator")]
See below. 
## The semiclassical limit and loop quantum gravity
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=17 "Edit section: The semiclassical limit and loop quantum gravity")]
The [Classical limit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_limit "Classical limit") is the ability of a physical theory to approximate classical mechanics. It is used with physical theories that predict non-classical behavior.[_[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_] Any candidate theory of quantum gravity must be able to reproduce Einstein's theory of [general relativity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity "General relativity") as a classical limit of a [quantum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quantum "Quantum") theory. This is not guaranteed because of a feature of quantum field theories which is that they have different sectors, these are analogous to the different phases that come about in the thermodynamical limit of statistical systems. Just as different phases are physically different, so are different sectors of a quantum field theory. It may turn out that LQG belongs to an unphysical sector – one in which one does not recover general relativity in the semiclassical limit or there might not be any physical sector. 
Moreover, the physical Hilbert space  H p h y s {\displaystyle H_{phys}} ![{\\displaystyle H_{phys}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/3782dfa1340583108b2483b353b7f7b3d1c51d94) must contain enough semiclassical states to guarantee that the quantum theory obtained can return to the classical theory when  ℏ → 0 {\displaystyle \hbar \to 0} ![{\\displaystyle \\hbar \\to 0}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/b2a3125ca5aa17bb14bf452daa5c3224c7470ab8) avoiding [quantum anomalies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quantum_anomalies "Quantum anomalies"); otherwise there will be restrictions on the physical Hilbert space that have no counterpart in the classical theory, implying that the quantum theory has fewer degrees of freedom than the classical theory. 
Theorems establishing the uniqueness of the loop representation as defined by Ashtekar et al. (i.e. a certain concrete realization of a Hilbert space and associated operators reproducing the correct loop algebra) have been given by two groups (Lewandowski, Okołów, Sahlmann and Thiemann;[[24]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTELewandowskiOko%C5%82%C3%B3wSahlmannThiemann2006703%E2%80%93733-24) and Christian Fleischhack[[25]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFleischhack2006061302-25)). Before this result was established it was not known whether there could be other examples of Hilbert spaces with operators invoking the same loop algebra – other realizations not equivalent to the one that had been used. These uniqueness theorems imply no others exist, so if LQG does not have the correct semiclassical limit then the theorems would mean the end of the loop representation of quantum gravity. 
### Difficulties and progress checking the semiclassical limit
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=18 "Edit section: Difficulties and progress checking the semiclassical limit")]
There are a number of difficulties in trying to establish LQG gives Einstein's theory of general relativity in the semiclassical limit: 
  1. There is no operator corresponding to infinitesimal spatial diffeomorphisms (it is not surprising that the theory has no generator of infinitesimal spatial 'translations' as it predicts spatial geometry has a discrete nature, compare to the situation in condensed matter). Instead it must be approximated by finite spatial diffeomorphisms and so the Poisson bracket structure of the classical theory is not exactly reproduced. This problem can be circumvented with the introduction of the so-called master constraint (see below).[[26]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThiemann2008Section_10.6-26)
  2. There is the problem of reconciling the discrete combinatorial nature of the quantum states with the continuous nature of the fields of the classical theory.
  3. There are serious difficulties arising from the structure of the Poisson brackets involving the spatial diffeomorphism and Hamiltonian constraints. In particular, the algebra of (smeared) Hamiltonian constraints does not close: It is proportional to a sum over infinitesimal spatial diffeomorphisms (which, as noted above, does not exist in the quantum theory) where the coefficients of proportionality are not constants but have non-trivial phase space dependence – as such it does not form a [Lie algebra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lie_algebra "Lie algebra"). However, the situation is improved by the introduction of the master constraint.[[26]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThiemann2008Section_10.6-26)
  4. The semiclassical machinery developed so far is only appropriate to non-graph-changing operators, however, Thiemann's Hamiltonian constraint is a graph-changing operator – the new graph it generates has degrees of freedom upon which the coherent state does not depend and so their quantum fluctuations are not suppressed. There is also the restriction, so far, that these coherent states are only defined at the Kinematic level, and now one has to lift them to the level of  H D i f f {\displaystyle {\mathcal {H}}_{Diff}} ![{\\displaystyle {\\mathcal {H}}_{Diff}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/9b68e8c11a92b1a8c5b25492ae4a6c0389871fe6) and  H P h y s {\displaystyle {\mathcal {H}}_{Phys}} ![{\\displaystyle {\\mathcal {H}}_{Phys}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/e9ddfcfc081c8b50a0b600f58d8663d892d41c06). It can be shown that Thiemann's Hamiltonian constraint is required to be graph-changing in order to resolve problem 3 in some sense. The master constraint algebra however is trivial and so the requirement that it be graph-changing can be lifted and indeed non-graph-changing master constraint operators have been defined. As far as is currently known, this problem is still out of reach.
  5. Formulating observables for classical general relativity is a formidable problem because of its non-linear nature and spacetime diffeomorphism invariance. A systematic approximation scheme to calculate observables has been recently developed.[[27]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDittrich20071891%E2%80%931927-27)[[28]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDittrich20066155%E2%80%936184-28)


Difficulties in trying to examine the semiclassical limit of the theory should not be confused with it having the wrong semiclassical limit. 
Concerning issue number 2 above, consider so-called [weave states](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Weave_states&action=edit&redlink=1 "Weave states \(page does not exist\)"). Ordinary measurements of geometric quantities are macroscopic, and Planckian discreteness is smoothed out. The fabric of a T-shirt is analogous: at a distance it is a smooth curved two-dimensional surface, but on closer inspection we see that it is actually composed of thousands of one-dimensional linked threads. The image of space given in LQG is similar. Consider a large spin network formed by a large number of nodes and links, each of [Planck scale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planck_scale "Planck scale"). Probed at a macroscopic scale, it appears as a three-dimensional continuous metric geometry. 
To make contact with low energy physics it is mandatory to develop approximation schemes both for the physical inner product and for Dirac observables; the spin foam models that have been intensively studied can be viewed as avenues toward approximation schemes for said physical inner product. 
Markopoulou, et al. adopted the idea of [noiseless subsystems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noiseless_subsystems "Noiseless subsystems") in an attempt to solve the problem of the low energy limit in background independent quantum gravity theories.[[29]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDreyerMarkopoulouSmolin20061%E2%80%9313-29)[[30]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKribsMarkopoulou2005-30) The idea has led to the possibility of matter of the [Standard Model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Model "Standard Model") being identified with emergent degrees of freedom from some versions of LQG (see section below: _LQG and related research programs_). 
As Wightman emphasized in the 1950s, in Minkowski QFTs the  n − {\displaystyle n-} ![{\\displaystyle n-}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/2d64b24ac9a5c67ef8e3ae80f109ce6e325d8464) point functions  W ( x 1 , … , x n ) = ⟨ 0 | ϕ ( x n ) … ϕ ( x 1 ) | 0 ⟩ , {\displaystyle W(x_{1},\dots ,x_{n})=\langle 0|\phi (x_{n})\dots \phi (x_{1})|0\rangle ,} ![{\\displaystyle W\(x_{1},\\dots ,x_{n}\)=\\langle 0|\\phi \(x_{n}\)\\dots \\phi \(x_{1}\)|0\\rangle ,}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/7b56d7e42c3fc7421fd1eaa02c2c96d0ca83e553) completely determine the theory. In particular, one can calculate the scattering amplitudes from these quantities. As explained below in the section on the _Background independent scattering amplitudes_ , in the background-independent context, the  n − {\displaystyle n-} ![{\\displaystyle n-}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/2d64b24ac9a5c67ef8e3ae80f109ce6e325d8464) point functions refer to a state and in gravity that state can naturally encode information about a specific geometry which can then appear in the expressions of these quantities. To leading order, LQG calculations have been shown to agree in an appropriate sense with the  n − {\displaystyle n-} ![{\\displaystyle n-}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/2d64b24ac9a5c67ef8e3ae80f109ce6e325d8464)point functions calculated in the effective low energy quantum general relativity. 
## Improved dynamics and the master constraint
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=19 "Edit section: Improved dynamics and the master constraint")]
Main articles: [Hamiltonian (quantum mechanics)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamiltonian_\(quantum_mechanics\) "Hamiltonian \(quantum mechanics\)"), [Hamiltonian constraint of LQG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamiltonian_constraint_of_LQG "Hamiltonian constraint of LQG"), and [Friedrichs extension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friedrichs_extension "Friedrichs extension")
### The master constraint
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=20 "Edit section: The master constraint")]
Thiemann's Master Constraint Programme for Loop Quantum Gravity (LQG) was proposed as a classically equivalent way to impose the infinite number of Hamiltonian constraint equations in terms of a single master constraint  M {\displaystyle M} ![{\\displaystyle M}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/f82cade9898ced02fdd08712e5f0c0151758a0dd), which involves the square of the constraints in question. An initial objection to the use of the master constraint was that on first sight it did not seem to encode information about the observables; because the Master constraint is quadratic in the constraint, when one computes its Poisson bracket with any quantity, the result is proportional to the constraint, therefore it vanishes when the constraints are imposed and as such does not select out particular phase space functions. However, it was realized that the condition  { O , { O , M } } M = 0 = 0 , {\displaystyle \\{O,\\{O,M\\}\\}_{M=0}=0,} ![{\\displaystyle \\{O,\\{O,M\\}\\}_{M=0}=0,}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/5c005c746270deff21ab20c0d9de0ebfe4b4a406) is where  O {\displaystyle O} ![{\\displaystyle O}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/9d70e1d0d87e2ef1092ea1ffe2923d9933ff18fc) is at least a twice differentiable function on phase space is equivalent to  O {\displaystyle O} ![{\\displaystyle O}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/9d70e1d0d87e2ef1092ea1ffe2923d9933ff18fc) being a weak Dirac observable with respect to the constraints in question. So the master constraint does capture information about the observables. Because of its significance this is known as the master equation.[[31]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThiemann2006a2211%E2%80%932247-31)
That the master constraint Poisson algebra is an honest Lie algebra opens the possibility of using a method, known as group averaging, in order to construct solutions of the infinite number of Hamiltonian constraints, a physical inner product thereon and [Dirac observables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dirac_observables "Dirac observables") via what is known as [refined algebraic quantization](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Refined_algebraic_quantization&action=edit&redlink=1 "Refined algebraic quantization \(page does not exist\)"), or RAQ.[[32]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-32)
### The quantum master constraint
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=21 "Edit section: The quantum master constraint")]
Define the quantum master constraint (regularisation issues aside) as  M ^ := ∫ d 3 x ( H det ( q ( x ) ) 4 ) ^ † ( x ) ( H det ( q ( x ) ) 4 ) ^ ( x ) . {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}:=\int d^{3}x{\widehat {\left({\frac {H}{\sqrt[{4}]{\det(q(x))}}}\right)}}^{\dagger }(x){\widehat {\left({\frac {H}{\sqrt[{4}]{\det(q(x))}}}\right)}}(x).} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}:=\\int d^{3}x{\\widehat {\\left\({\\frac {H}{\\sqrt\[{4}\]{\\det\(q\(x\)\)}}}\\right\)}}^{\\dagger }\(x\){\\widehat {\\left\({\\frac {H}{\\sqrt\[{4}\]{\\det\(q\(x\)\)}}}\\right\)}}\(x\).}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/1af65b1a963edd80a66d252d9d1a98e0bfa020a4)
Obviously,  ( H det ( q ( x ) ) 4 ) ^ ( x ) Ψ = 0 {\displaystyle {\widehat {\left({\frac {H}{\sqrt[{4}]{\det(q(x))}}}\right)}}(x)\Psi =0} ![{\\displaystyle {\\widehat {\\left\({\\frac {H}{\\sqrt\[{4}\]{\\det\(q\(x\)\)}}}\\right\)}}\(x\)\\Psi =0}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/d2187321ad47a3f09f6b75287740c336aede43d0) for all  x {\displaystyle x} ![{\\displaystyle x}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/87f9e315fd7e2ba406057a97300593c4802b53e4) implies  M ^ Ψ = 0 {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}\Psi =0} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}\\Psi =0}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/3d242015c8caf2c52b927bcd4dc978ee5c0767a2). Conversely, if  M ^ Ψ = 0 {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}\Psi =0} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}\\Psi =0}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/3d242015c8caf2c52b927bcd4dc978ee5c0767a2) then  0 = ⟨ Ψ , M ^ Ψ ⟩ = ∫ d 3 x ‖ ( H det ( q ( x ) ) 4 ) ^ ( x ) Ψ ‖ 2 E q 4 {\displaystyle 0=\left\langle \Psi ,{\hat {M}}\Psi \right\rangle =\int d^{3}x\left\|{\widehat {\left({\frac {H}{\sqrt[{4}]{\det(q(x))}}}\right)}}(x)\Psi \right\|^{2}\qquad Eq\;4} ![{\\displaystyle 0=\\left\\langle \\Psi ,{\\hat {M}}\\Psi \\right\\rangle =\\int d^{3}x\\left\\|{\\widehat {\\left\({\\frac {H}{\\sqrt\[{4}\]{\\det\(q\(x\)\)}}}\\right\)}}\(x\)\\Psi \\right\\|^{2}\\qquad Eq\\;4}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/3b810fba7255d34a82966ddb6011fae6ace48c05) implies  ( H det ( q ( x ) ) 4 ) ^ ( x ) Ψ = 0. {\displaystyle {\widehat {\left({\frac {H}{\sqrt[{4}]{\det(q(x))}}}\right)}}(x)\Psi =0.} ![{\\displaystyle {\\widehat {\\left\({\\frac {H}{\\sqrt\[{4}\]{\\det\(q\(x\)\)}}}\\right\)}}\(x\)\\Psi =0.}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/827f156e4031b6346948b6b6fe4099ff9f6f2143)
First compute the matrix elements of the would-be operator  M ^ {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/73c0360d83c956df7c381c88261a060905be4b77), that is, the quadratic form  Q M {\displaystyle Q_{M}} ![{\\displaystyle Q_{M}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a6d7de399522ce27c87ae7d581e95c571c7ba6b6).  Q M {\displaystyle Q_{M}} ![{\\displaystyle Q_{M}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a6d7de399522ce27c87ae7d581e95c571c7ba6b6) is a graph changing, diffeomorphism invariant quadratic form that cannot exist on the kinematic Hilbert space  H K i n {\displaystyle H_{Kin}} ![{\\displaystyle H_{Kin}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a45164b316e11825311dd6cfa739b7fad0fb1079), and must be defined on  H D i f f {\displaystyle H_{Diff}} ![{\\displaystyle H_{Diff}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/de73822a242d05c43560da9dd2cf0e4914114b3c). Since the master constraint operator  M ^ {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/73c0360d83c956df7c381c88261a060905be4b77) is [densely defined](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Densely_defined "Densely defined") on  H D i f f {\displaystyle H_{Diff}} ![{\\displaystyle H_{Diff}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/de73822a242d05c43560da9dd2cf0e4914114b3c), then  M ^ {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/73c0360d83c956df7c381c88261a060905be4b77) is a positive and [symmetric operator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symmetric_operator "Symmetric operator") in  H D i f f {\displaystyle H_{Diff}} ![{\\displaystyle H_{Diff}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/de73822a242d05c43560da9dd2cf0e4914114b3c). Therefore, the quadratic form  Q M {\displaystyle Q_{M}} ![{\\displaystyle Q_{M}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a6d7de399522ce27c87ae7d581e95c571c7ba6b6) associated with  M ^ {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/73c0360d83c956df7c381c88261a060905be4b77) is [closable](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Closable&action=edit&redlink=1 "Closable \(page does not exist\)"). The closure of  Q M {\displaystyle Q_{M}} ![{\\displaystyle Q_{M}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a6d7de399522ce27c87ae7d581e95c571c7ba6b6) is the quadratic form of a unique [self-adjoint operator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-adjoint_operator "Self-adjoint operator") M ¯ ^ {\displaystyle {\hat {\overline {M}}}} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {\\overline {M}}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/9a0e4124629230fdaad38394d7b94a28a60dfd4e), called the [Friedrichs extension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friedrichs_extension "Friedrichs extension") of  M ^ {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/73c0360d83c956df7c381c88261a060905be4b77). We relabel  M ¯ ^ {\displaystyle {\hat {\overline {M}}}} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {\\overline {M}}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/9a0e4124629230fdaad38394d7b94a28a60dfd4e) as  M ^ {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/73c0360d83c956df7c381c88261a060905be4b77) for simplicity. 
Note that the presence of an inner product, viz Eq 4, means there are no superfluous solutions i.e. there are no  Ψ {\displaystyle \Psi } ![{\\displaystyle \\Psi }](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/f5471531a3fe80741a839bc98d49fae862a6439a) such that  ( H det ( q ( x ) ) 4 ) ^ ( x ) Ψ ≠ 0 , {\displaystyle {\widehat {\left({\frac {H}{\sqrt[{4}]{\det(q(x))}}}\right)}}(x)\Psi \not =0,} ![{\\displaystyle {\\widehat {\\left\({\\frac {H}{\\sqrt\[{4}\]{\\det\(q\(x\)\)}}}\\right\)}}\(x\)\\Psi \\not =0,}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/e83fa137e5ca26e6fbd0545649710d9a9b7353e4) but for which  M ^ Ψ = 0 {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}\Psi =0} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}\\Psi =0}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/3d242015c8caf2c52b927bcd4dc978ee5c0767a2). 
It is also possible to construct a quadratic form  Q M E {\displaystyle Q_{M_{E}}} ![{\\displaystyle Q_{M_{E}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/b862670d574c598d072b3786c6cb52917faf9e64) for what is called the extended master constraint (discussed below) on  H K i n {\displaystyle H_{Kin}} ![{\\displaystyle H_{Kin}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a45164b316e11825311dd6cfa739b7fad0fb1079) which also involves the weighted integral of the square of the spatial diffeomorphism constraint (this is possible because  Q M E {\displaystyle Q_{M_{E}}} ![{\\displaystyle Q_{M_{E}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/b862670d574c598d072b3786c6cb52917faf9e64) is not graph changing). 
The spectrum of the master constraint may not contain zero due to normal or factor ordering effects which are finite but similar in nature to the infinite vacuum energies of background-dependent quantum field theories. In this case it turns out to be physically correct to replace  M ^ {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/73c0360d83c956df7c381c88261a060905be4b77) with  M ^ ′ := M ^ − min ( s p e c ( M ^ ) ) 1 ^ {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}':={\hat {M}}-\min(spec({\hat {M}})){\hat {1}}} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}':={\\hat {M}}-\\min\(spec\({\\hat {M}}\)\){\\hat {1}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/ba977aa5be2a2033188444548b6e25bfa6c17ee6) provided that the "normal ordering constant" vanishes in the classical limit, that is,  lim ℏ → 0 min ( s p e c ( M ^ ) ) = 0 , {\displaystyle \lim _{\hbar \to 0}\min(spec({\hat {M}}))=0,} ![{\\displaystyle \\lim _{\\hbar \\to 0}\\min\(spec\({\\hat {M}}\)\)=0,}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/766d41080708855d45110d2f4430801521a8bea7) so that  M ^ ′ {\displaystyle {\hat {M}}'} ![{\\displaystyle {\\hat {M}}'}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/0b74b4b8f6be04df8e2d6893718f32877ed09f86) is a valid quantisation of  M {\displaystyle M} ![{\\displaystyle M}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/f82cade9898ced02fdd08712e5f0c0151758a0dd). 
### Testing the master constraint
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=22 "Edit section: Testing the master constraint")]
The constraints in their primitive form are rather singular, this was the reason for integrating them over test functions to obtain smeared constraints. However, it would appear that the equation for the master constraint, given above, is even more singular involving the product of two primitive constraints (although integrated over space). Squaring the constraint is dangerous as it could lead to worsened ultraviolet behaviour of the corresponding operator and hence the master constraint programme must be approached with care. 
In doing so the master constraint programme has been satisfactorily tested in a number of model systems with non-trivial constraint algebras, free and interacting field theories.[[33]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDittrichThiemann2006a1025%E2%80%931066-33)[[34]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDittrichThiemann2006b1067%E2%80%931088-34)[[35]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDittrichThiemann2006c1089%E2%80%931120-35)[[36]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDittrichThiemann2006d1121%E2%80%931142-36)[[37]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDittrichThiemann2006e1143%E2%80%931162-37) The master constraint for LQG was established as a genuine positive self-adjoint operator and the physical Hilbert space of LQG was shown to be non-empty,[[38]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThiemann2006b2249%E2%80%932265-38) a consistency test LQG must pass to be a viable theory of quantum general relativity. 
### Applications of the master constraint
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=23 "Edit section: Applications of the master constraint")]
The master constraint has been employed in attempts to approximate the physical inner product and define more rigorous path integrals.[[39]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBahrThiemann20072109%E2%80%932138-39)[[40]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHanThiemann2010a225019-40)[[41]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHanThiemann2010b092501-41)[[42]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHan2010215009-42)
The Consistent Discretizations approach to LQG,[[43]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGambiniPullin2009035002-43)[[44]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGambiniPullin2011Section_10.2.2-44) is an application of the master constraint program to construct the physical Hilbert space of the canonical theory. 
### Spin foam from the master constraint
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=24 "Edit section: Spin foam from the master constraint")]
The master constraint is easily generalized to incorporate the other constraints. It is then referred to as the extended master constraint, denoted  M E {\displaystyle M_{E}} ![{\\displaystyle M_{E}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/d8512d0383278c80083d6da1c71610c10f0baac0). We can define the extended master constraint which imposes both the Hamiltonian constraint and spatial diffeomorphism constraint as a single operator,  M E = ∫ Σ d 3 x H ( x ) 2 − q a b V a ( x ) V b ( x ) det ( q ) . {\displaystyle M_{E}=\int _{\Sigma }d^{3}x{H(x)^{2}-q^{ab}V_{a}(x)V_{b}(x) \over {\sqrt {\det(q)}}}.} ![{\\displaystyle M_{E}=\\int _{\\Sigma }d^{3}x{H\(x\)^{2}-q^{ab}V_{a}\(x\)V_{b}\(x\) \\over {\\sqrt {\\det\(q\)}}}.}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/29b35ffc687fe99a3707f5fadefb7305f029f54a)
Setting this single constraint to zero is equivalent to  H ( x ) = 0 {\displaystyle H(x)=0} ![{\\displaystyle H\(x\)=0}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/f35c3e4050803201707346f740a2544268d09ca1) and  V a ( x ) = 0 {\displaystyle V_{a}(x)=0} ![{\\displaystyle V_{a}\(x\)=0}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/01b4a5a8f50102cc0dd05dc4833822dbf40732e3) for all  x {\displaystyle x} ![{\\displaystyle x}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/87f9e315fd7e2ba406057a97300593c4802b53e4) in  Σ {\displaystyle \Sigma } ![{\\displaystyle \\Sigma }](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/9e1f558f53cda207614abdf90162266c70bc5c1e). This constraint implements the spatial diffeomorphism and Hamiltonian constraint at the same time on the Kinematic Hilbert space. The physical inner product is then defined as  ⟨ ϕ , ψ ⟩ Phys = lim T → ∞ ⟨ ϕ , ∫ − T T d t e i t M ^ E ψ ⟩ {\displaystyle \langle \phi ,\psi \rangle _{\text{Phys}}=\lim _{T\to \infty }\left\langle \phi ,\int _{-T}^{T}dte^{it{\hat {M}}_{E}}\psi \right\rangle } ![{\\displaystyle \\langle \\phi ,\\psi \\rangle _{\\text{Phys}}=\\lim _{T\\to \\infty }\\left\\langle \\phi ,\\int _{-T}^{T}dte^{it{\\hat {M}}_{E}}\\psi \\right\\rangle }](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/043b829f693ecaaba48b1b404ca0d44804ec6a3f) (as  δ ( M E ^ ) = lim T → ∞ ∫ − T T d t e i t M ^ E {\textstyle \delta ({\hat {M_{E}}})=\lim _{T\to \infty }\int _{-T}^{T}dte^{it{\hat {M}}_{E}}} ![{\\textstyle \\delta \({\\hat {M_{E}}}\)=\\lim _{T\\to \\infty }\\int _{-T}^{T}dte^{it{\\hat {M}}_{E}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a16394f90a768ebf31dbdde182a75fcadaa48ed6)). A spin foam representation of this expression is obtained by splitting the  t {\displaystyle t} ![{\\displaystyle t}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/65658b7b223af9e1acc877d848888ecdb4466560)-parameter in discrete steps and writing  e i t M ^ E = lim n → ∞ [ e i t M ^ E / n ] n = lim n → ∞ [ 1 + i t M ^ E / n ] n . {\displaystyle e^{it{\hat {M}}_{E}}=\lim _{n\to \infty }\left[e^{it{\hat {M}}_{E}/n}\right]^{n}=\lim _{n\to \infty }[1+it{\hat {M}}_{E}/n]^{n}.} ![{\\displaystyle e^{it{\\hat {M}}_{E}}=\\lim _{n\\to \\infty }\\left\[e^{it{\\hat {M}}_{E}/n}\\right\]^{n}=\\lim _{n\\to \\infty }\[1+it{\\hat {M}}_{E}/n\]^{n}.}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/341f6f48274742453ba5a1584f62b289fae730ac)
The spin foam description then follows from the application of  [ 1 + i t M ^ E / n ] {\displaystyle [1+it{\hat {M}}_{E}/n]} ![{\\displaystyle \[1+it{\\hat {M}}_{E}/n\]}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/5c76e7076d76b46808e2d202f39d6a98b8c9ab74) on a spin network resulting in a linear combination of new spin networks whose graph and labels have been modified. Obviously an approximation is made by truncating the value of  n {\displaystyle n} ![{\\displaystyle n}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a601995d55609f2d9f5e233e36fbe9ea26011b3b) to some finite integer. An advantage of the extended master constraint is that we are working at the kinematic level and so far it is only here we have access semiclassical coherent states. Moreover, one can find none graph changing versions of this master constraint operator, which are the only type of operators appropriate for these coherent states. 
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=25 "Edit section: Algebraic quantum gravity \(AQG\)")]
The master constraint programme has evolved into a fully combinatorial treatment of gravity known as algebraic quantum gravity (AQG).[[45]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGieselThiemann2007a2465%E2%80%932498-45) The non-graph changing master constraint operator is adapted in the framework of algebraic quantum gravity. While AQG is inspired by LQG, it differs drastically from it because in AQG there is fundamentally no topology or differential structure – it is background independent in a more generalized sense and could possibly have something to say about topology change. In this new formulation of quantum gravity AQG semiclassical states always control the fluctuations of all present degrees of freedom. This makes the AQG semiclassical analysis superior over that of LQG, and progress has been made in establishing it has the correct semiclassical limit and providing contact with familiar low energy physics.[[46]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGieselThiemann2007b2499%E2%80%932564-46)[[47]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGieselThiemann2007c2565%E2%80%932588-47)
## Physical applications of LQG
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=26 "Edit section: Physical applications of LQG")]
### Black hole entropy
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=27 "Edit section: Black hole entropy")]
Main articles: [Black hole thermodynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_hole_thermodynamics "Black hole thermodynamics"), [Isolated horizon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isolated_horizon "Isolated horizon"), and [Immirzi parameter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immirzi_parameter "Immirzi parameter")
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/Black_Hole_Merger.jpg/330px-Black_Hole_Merger.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Black_Hole_Merger.jpg)An artist depiction of two [black holes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_hole "Black hole") merging, a process in which the [laws of thermodynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laws_of_thermodynamics "Laws of thermodynamics") are upheld
Black hole thermodynamics is the area of study that seeks to reconcile the [laws of thermodynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laws_of_thermodynamics "Laws of thermodynamics") with the existence of [black hole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_hole "Black hole") [event horizons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Event_horizon "Event horizon"). The [no hair conjecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_hair_theorem "No hair theorem") of general relativity states that a black hole is characterized only by its [mass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass "Mass"), its [charge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charge_\(physics\) "Charge \(physics\)"), and its [angular momentum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angular_momentum "Angular momentum"); hence, it has no [entropy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entropy "Entropy"). It appears, then, that one can violate the [second law of thermodynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_law_of_thermodynamics "Second law of thermodynamics") by dropping an object with nonzero entropy into a black hole.[[48]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBousso2002825%E2%80%93874-48) Work by [Stephen Hawking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Hawking "Stephen Hawking") and [Jacob Bekenstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Bekenstein "Jacob Bekenstein") showed that the second law of thermodynamics can be preserved by assigning to each black hole a _black-hole entropy_ S BH = k B A 4 ℓ P 2 , {\displaystyle S_{\text{BH}}={\frac {k_{\text{B}}A}{4\ell _{\text{P}}^{2}}},} ![{\\displaystyle S_{\\text{BH}}={\\frac {k_{\\text{B}}A}{4\\ell _{\\text{P}}^{2}}},}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/33b6c81ff01d28b873ee6de59c03fd6de69f09f4) where  A {\displaystyle A} ![{\\displaystyle A}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/7daff47fa58cdfd29dc333def748ff5fa4c923e3) is the area of the hole's event horizon,  k B {\displaystyle k_{\text{B}}} ![{\\displaystyle k_{\\text{B}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/9582c7c795d2def2c061f0dfa3a6f0fb3dd2de44) is the [Boltzmann constant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boltzmann_constant "Boltzmann constant"), and  ℓ P = G ℏ / c 3 {\textstyle \ell _{\text{P}}={\sqrt {G\hbar /c^{3}}}} ![{\\textstyle \\ell _{\\text{P}}={\\sqrt {G\\hbar /c^{3}}}}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/c128331ddd307287758b86178052e9ca5069dca7) is the Planck length.[[49]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMajumdar1998147-49) The fact that the black hole entropy is also the maximal entropy that can be obtained by the [Bekenstein bound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bekenstein_bound "Bekenstein bound") (wherein the Bekenstein bound becomes an equality) was the main observation that led to the [holographic principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holographic_principle "Holographic principle").[[48]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBousso2002825%E2%80%93874-48)
An oversight in the application of the [no-hair theorem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No-hair_theorem "No-hair theorem") is the assumption that the relevant degrees of freedom accounting for the entropy of the black hole must be classical in nature; what if they were purely quantum mechanical instead and had non-zero entropy? This is what is realized in the LQG derivation of black hole entropy, and can be seen as a consequence of its background-independence – the classical black hole spacetime comes about from the semiclassical limit of the [quantum state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quantum_state "Quantum state") of the gravitational field, but there are many quantum states that have the same semiclassical limit. Specifically, in LQG[[50]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-ReferenceA-50) it is possible to associate a quantum geometrical interpretation to the microstates: These are the quantum geometries of the horizon which are consistent with the area,  A {\displaystyle A} ![{\\displaystyle A}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/7daff47fa58cdfd29dc333def748ff5fa4c923e3), of the black hole and the topology of the horizon (i.e. spherical). LQG offers a geometric explanation of the finiteness of the entropy and of the proportionality of the area of the horizon.[[51]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTERovelli19963288%E2%80%933291-51)[[52]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAshtekarBaezCorichiKrasnov1998904%E2%80%93907-52) These calculations have been generalized to rotating black holes.[[53]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAshtekarEngleBroeck2005L27-53)
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/9d/LQG_black_hole_Horizon.jpg/330px-LQG_black_hole_Horizon.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:LQG_black_hole_Horizon.jpg)Representation of quantum geometries of the horizon. Polymer excitations in the bulk puncture the horizon, endowing it with quantized area. Intrinsically the horizon is flat except at punctures where it acquires a quantized [deficit angle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defect_\(geometry\) "Defect \(geometry\)") or quantized amount of curvature. These deficit angles add up to  4 π {\displaystyle 4\pi } ![{\\displaystyle 4\\pi }](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/057444bf35a0c22b19bcae1ef06e06ecdf8abe56).
It is possible to derive, from the covariant formulation of full quantum theory ([Spinfoam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spinfoam "Spinfoam")) the correct relation between energy and area (1st law), the [Unruh temperature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unruh_temperature "Unruh temperature") and the distribution that yields Hawking entropy.[[54]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBianchi2012-54) The calculation makes use of the notion of [dynamical horizon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynamical_horizon "Dynamical horizon") and is done for non-extremal black holes. 
A recent success of the theory in this direction is the computation of the [entropy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entropy "Entropy") of all non singular black holes directly from theory and independent of [Immirzi parameter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immirzi_parameter "Immirzi parameter").[[54]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBianchi2012-54)[[55]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFroddenGhoshPerez2013121503-55) The result is the expected formula  S = A / 4 {\displaystyle S=A/4} ![{\\displaystyle S=A/4}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/61d46efde3de258dc36bc6137cdc14ffa817b80f), where  S {\displaystyle S} ![{\\displaystyle S}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/4611d85173cd3b508e67077d4a1252c9c05abca2) is the entropy and  A {\displaystyle A} ![{\\displaystyle A}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/7daff47fa58cdfd29dc333def748ff5fa4c923e3) the area of the black hole, derived by Bekenstein and Hawking on heuristic grounds. This is the only known derivation of this formula from a fundamental theory, for the case of generic non singular black holes. Older attempts at this calculation had difficulties. The problem was that although Loop quantum gravity predicted that the entropy of a black hole is proportional to the area of the event horizon, the result depended on a crucial free parameter in the theory, the above-mentioned Immirzi parameter. However, there is no known computation of the Immirzi parameter, so it was fixed by demanding agreement with Bekenstein and Hawking's calculation of the [black hole entropy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_hole_thermodynamics "Black hole thermodynamics"). 
### Hawking radiation in loop quantum gravity
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=28 "Edit section: Hawking radiation in loop quantum gravity")]
Main article: [Hawking radiation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawking_radiation "Hawking radiation")
A detailed study of the quantum geometry of a black hole horizon has been made using loop quantum gravity.[[52]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAshtekarBaezCorichiKrasnov1998904%E2%80%93907-52) Loop-quantization does not reproduce the result for [black hole entropy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_hole_entropy "Black hole entropy") originally discovered by Bekenstein and Hawking, unless one chooses the value of the [Immirzi parameter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immirzi_parameter "Immirzi parameter") to cancel out another constant that arises in the derivation. However, it led to the computation of higher-order corrections to the entropy and radiation of black holes. 
Based on the fluctuations of the horizon area, a quantum black hole exhibits deviations from the Hawking spectrum that would be observable were [X-rays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X-ray "X-ray") from Hawking radiation of evaporating [primordial black holes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primordial_black_holes "Primordial black holes") to be observed.[[56]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnsari2007179%E2%80%93212-56) The quantum effects are centered at a set of discrete and unblended frequencies highly pronounced on top of Hawking radiation spectrum.[[57]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnsari2008635%E2%80%93644-57)
### Planck star
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=29 "Edit section: Planck star")]
Main articles: [Planck star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planck_star "Planck star"), [black hole firewall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_hole_firewall "Black hole firewall"), and [black hole information paradox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_hole_information_paradox "Black hole information paradox")
In 2014 Carlo Rovelli and [Francesca Vidotto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesca_Vidotto "Francesca Vidotto") proposed that there is a [Planck star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planck_star "Planck star") inside every black hole.[[58]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTERovelliVidotto20141442026-58) Based on LQG, the theory states that as stars are collapsing into black holes, the energy density reaches the Planck energy density, causing a repulsive force that creates a star. Furthermore, the existence of such a star would resolve the [black hole firewall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_hole_firewall "Black hole firewall") and [black hole information paradox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_hole_information_paradox "Black hole information paradox"). 
### Loop quantum cosmology
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=30 "Edit section: Loop quantum cosmology")]
Main articles: [Loop quantum cosmology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_cosmology "Loop quantum cosmology"), [Big Bounce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Bounce "Big Bounce"), and [inflation (cosmology)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inflation_\(cosmology\) "Inflation \(cosmology\)")
The popular and technical literature makes extensive references to the LQG-related topic of loop quantum cosmology. LQC was mainly developed by Martin Bojowald. It was popularized in _[Scientific American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_American "Scientific American")_ for predicting a [Big Bounce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Bounce "Big Bounce") prior to the [Big Bang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Bang "Big Bang").[[59]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBojowald2008-59) Loop quantum cosmology (LQC) is a symmetry-reduced model of classical general relativity quantized using methods that mimic those of loop quantum gravity (LQG) that predicts a "quantum bridge" between contracting and expanding cosmological branches. 
Achievements of LQC have been the resolution of the big bang [singularity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singularity_\(mathematics\) "Singularity \(mathematics\)"), the prediction of a Big Bounce, and a natural mechanism for [inflation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inflation_\(cosmology\) "Inflation \(cosmology\)"). 
LQC models share features of LQG and so is a useful toy model. However, the results obtained are subject to the usual restriction that a truncated classical theory, then quantized, might not display the true behaviour of the full theory due to artificial suppression of degrees of freedom that might have large quantum fluctuations in the full theory. It has been argued that singularity avoidance in LQC are by mechanisms only available in these restrictive models and that singularity avoidance in the full theory can still be obtained but by a more subtle feature of LQG.[[60]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrunnemannThiemann2006a1395%E2%80%931428-60)[[61]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrunnemannThiemann2006b1429%E2%80%931484-61)
### Loop quantum gravity phenomenology
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=31 "Edit section: Loop quantum gravity phenomenology")]
Quantum gravity effects are difficult to measure because the Planck length is so small. However recently physicists, such as Jack Palmer, have started to consider the possibility of measuring quantum gravity effects mostly from astrophysical observations and gravitational wave detectors. The energy of those fluctuations at scales this small cause space-perturbations which are visible at higher scales. 
### Background-independent scattering amplitudes
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=32 "Edit section: Background-independent scattering amplitudes")]
Main article: [Scattering amplitude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scattering_amplitude "Scattering amplitude")
Loop quantum gravity is formulated in a background-independent language. No spacetime is assumed a priori, but rather it is built up by the states of theory themselves – however scattering amplitudes are derived from  n {\displaystyle n} ![{\\displaystyle n}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a601995d55609f2d9f5e233e36fbe9ea26011b3b)-point functions ([Correlation function](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Correlation_function_\(quantum_field_theory\) "Correlation function \(quantum field theory\)")) and these, formulated in conventional quantum field theory, are functions of points of a background spacetime. The relation between the background-independent formalism and the conventional formalism of quantum field theory on a given spacetime is not obvious, and it is not obvious how to recover low-energy quantities from the full background-independent theory. One would like to derive the  n {\displaystyle n} ![{\\displaystyle n}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a601995d55609f2d9f5e233e36fbe9ea26011b3b)-point functions of the theory from the background-independent formalism, in order to compare them with the standard perturbative expansion of quantum general relativity and therefore check that loop quantum gravity yields the correct low-energy limit. 
A strategy for addressing this problem has been suggested;[[62]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEModestoRovelli2005191301-62) by studying the boundary amplitude, namely a path integral over a finite spacetime region, seen as a function of the boundary value of the field.[[63]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOeckl2003a318%E2%80%93324-63)[[64]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOeckl2003b5371%E2%80%935380-64) In conventional quantum field theory, this boundary amplitude is well–defined[[65]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEConradyRovelli20044037-65)[[66]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDoplicher2004064037-66) and codes the physical information of the theory; it does so in quantum gravity as well, but in a fully background–independent manner.[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEConradyDoplicherOecklRovelli2004064019-67) A generally covariant definition of  n {\displaystyle n} ![{\\displaystyle n}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a601995d55609f2d9f5e233e36fbe9ea26011b3b)-point functions can then be based on the idea that the distance between physical points – arguments of the  n {\displaystyle n} ![{\\displaystyle n}](https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a601995d55609f2d9f5e233e36fbe9ea26011b3b)-point function is determined by the state of the gravitational field on the boundary of the spacetime region considered. 
Progress has been made in calculating background-independent scattering amplitudes this way with the use of spin foams. This is a way to extract physical information from the theory. Claims to have reproduced the correct behaviour for graviton scattering amplitudes and to have recovered classical gravity have been made. "We have calculated Newton's law starting from a world with no space and no time." – Carlo Rovelli. 
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=33 "Edit section: Gravitons, string theory, supersymmetry, extra dimensions in LQG")]
Main articles: [Graviton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graviton "Graviton"), [string theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/String_theory "String theory"), [supersymmetry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supersymmetry "Supersymmetry"), [Kaluza–Klein theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaluza%E2%80%93Klein_theory "Kaluza–Klein theory"), and [supergravity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supergravity "Supergravity")
Some quantum theories of gravity posit a spin-2 quantum field that is quantized, giving rise to gravitons. In string theory, one generally starts with quantized excitations on top of a classically fixed background. This theory is thus described as background dependent. Particles like photons as well as changes in the spacetime geometry (gravitons) are both described as excitations on the string worldsheet. The background dependence of string theory can have physical consequences, such as determining the number of quark generations. In contrast, loop quantum gravity, like general relativity, is manifestly background independent, eliminating the background required in string theory. Loop quantum gravity, like string theory, also aims to overcome the nonrenormalizable divergences of quantum field theories. 
LQG does not introduce a background and excitations living on such a background, so LQG does not use gravitons as building blocks. Instead one expects that one may recover a kind of semiclassical limit or weak field limit where something like "gravitons" will show up again. In contrast, gravitons play a key role in string theory where they are among the first (massless) level of excitations of a superstring. 
LQG differs from string theory in that it is formulated in 3 and 4 dimensions and without supersymmetry or [Kaluza–Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaluza%E2%80%93Klein_theory "Kaluza–Klein theory") extra dimensions, while the latter requires both to be true. There is no experimental evidence to date that confirms string theory's predictions of supersymmetry and Kaluza–Klein extra dimensions. In a 2003 paper "A Dialog on Quantum Gravity",[[68]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTERovelli20031509%E2%80%931528-68) Carlo Rovelli regards the fact LQG is formulated in 4 dimensions and without supersymmetry as a strength of the theory as it represents the most [parsimonious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parsimonious "Parsimonious") explanation, consistent with current experimental results, over its rival string/M-theory. Proponents of string theory will often point to the fact that, among other things, it demonstrably reproduces the established theories of general relativity and quantum field theory in the appropriate limits, which loop quantum gravity has struggled to do. In that sense string theory's connection to established physics may be considered more reliable and less speculative, at the mathematical level. Loop quantum gravity has nothing to say about the matter (fermions) in the universe. 
Since LQG has been formulated in 4 dimensions (with and without supersymmetry), and M-theory requires supersymmetry and 11 dimensions, a direct comparison between the two has not been possible. It is possible to extend mainstream LQG formalism to higher-dimensional supergravity, general relativity with supersymmetry and Kaluza–Klein extra dimensions should experimental evidence establish their existence. It would therefore be desirable to have higher-dimensional Supergravity loop quantizations at one's disposal in order to compare these approaches. A series of papers have been published attempting this.[[69]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodendorferThiemannThurn2013a045001-69)[[70]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodendorferThiemannThurn2013b045002-70)[[71]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodendorferThiemannThurn2013c045003-71)[[72]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodendorferThiemannThurn2013d045004-72)[[73]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodendorferThiemannThurn2013e045005-73)[[74]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodendorferThiemannThurn2012205-74)[[75]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodendorferThiemannThurn2013f045006-75)[[76]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodendorferThiemannThurn2013g045007-76) Most recently, Thiemann (and alumni) have made progress toward calculating black hole entropy for supergravity in higher dimensions. It will be useful to compare these results to the corresponding super string calculations.[[77]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodendorferThiemannThurn2014055002-77)[[78]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBodendorfer2013887%E2%80%93891-78)
## LQG and related research programs
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=34 "Edit section: LQG and related research programs")]
Main articles: [Noncommutative geometry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noncommutative_geometry "Noncommutative geometry"), [twistor theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twistor_theory "Twistor theory"), [entropic gravity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entropic_gravity "Entropic gravity"), [asymptotic safety in quantum gravity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asymptotic_safety_in_quantum_gravity "Asymptotic safety in quantum gravity"), [causal dynamical triangulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Causal_dynamical_triangulation "Causal dynamical triangulation"), and [group field theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_field_theory "Group field theory")
Several research groups have attempted to combine LQG with other research programs: Johannes Aastrup, Jesper M. Grimstrup et al. research combines [noncommutative geometry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noncommutative_geometry "Noncommutative geometry") with canonical quantum gravity and Ashtekar variables,[[79]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAastrup2012018-79) Laurent Freidel, Simone Speziale, et al., [spinors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spinors "Spinors") and [twistor theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twistor_theory "Twistor theory") with loop quantum gravity,[[80]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreidelSpeziale2010084041-80)[[81]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTESpezialeWieland2012124023-81) and Lee Smolin et al. with Verlinde [entropic gravity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entropic_gravity "Entropic gravity") and loop gravity.[[82]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTESmolin2010-82) Stephon Alexander, Antonino Marciano and Lee Smolin have attempted to explain the origins of [weak force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weak_force "Weak force") chirality in terms of Ashketar's variables, which describe gravity as chiral,[[83]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlexanderMarcian%C3%B2Smolin2014065017-83) and LQG with [Yang–Mills theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yang%E2%80%93Mills_theory "Yang–Mills theory") fields[[84]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlexanderMarcian%C3%B2Tacchi2012330-84) in four dimensions. [Sundance Bilson-Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sundance_Bilson-Thompson "Sundance Bilson-Thompson"), Hackett et al.,[[85]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBilson-ThompsonMarkopoulouSmolin20073975%E2%80%933994-85)[[86]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBilson-Thompson2012014-86) has attempted to introduce the standard model via LQGs degrees of freedom as an emergent property (by employing the idea of [noiseless subsystems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noiseless_subsystems "Noiseless subsystems"), a notion introduced in a more general situation for constrained systems by [Fotini Markopoulou-Kalamara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fotini_Markopoulou-Kalamara "Fotini Markopoulou-Kalamara") et al.)[[87]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-87)
Furthermore, LQG has drawn philosophical comparisons with [causal dynamical triangulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Causal_dynamical_triangulation "Causal dynamical triangulation")[[88]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPITP:_Renate_Loll-88) and [asymptotically safe gravity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asymptotically_safe_gravity "Asymptotically safe gravity"),[[89]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBianchi2010-89) and the spinfoam with [group field theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_field_theory "Group field theory") and [AdS/CFT correspondence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AdS/CFT_correspondence "AdS/CFT correspondence").[[90]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreidel2008-90) Smolin and Wen have suggested combining LQG with [string-net liquid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/String-net_liquid "String-net liquid"), [tensors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tensor "Tensor"), and Smolin and [Fotini Markopoulou-Kalamara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fotini_Markopoulou-Kalamara "Fotini Markopoulou-Kalamara") [quantum graphity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quantum_graphity "Quantum graphity"). There is the consistent discretizations approach. Also, Pullin and [Gambini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodolfo_Gambini "Rodolfo Gambini") provide a framework to connect the [path integral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Functional_integration "Functional integration") and canonical approaches to quantum gravity. They may help reconcile the spin foam and canonical loop representation approaches. Recent research by Chris Duston and [Matilde Marcolli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matilde_Marcolli "Matilde Marcolli") introduces [topology change](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Topology_change&action=edit&redlink=1 "Topology change \(page does not exist\)") via topspin networks.[[91]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuston2013-91)
## Problems and comparisons with alternative approaches
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=35 "Edit section: Problems and comparisons with alternative approaches")]
Main article: [List of unsolved problems in physics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_unsolved_problems_in_physics "List of unsolved problems in physics")
Some of the major unsolved problems in physics are theoretical, meaning that existing theories seem incapable of explaining a certain observed phenomenon or experimental result. The others are experimental, meaning that there is a difficulty in creating an experiment to test a proposed theory or investigate a phenomenon in greater detail. 
Many of these problems apply to LQG, including: 
  * Can quantum mechanics and general relativity be realized as a fully consistent theory (perhaps as a quantum field theory)?
  * Is spacetime fundamentally continuous or discrete?
  * Would a consistent theory involve a force mediated by a hypothetical graviton, or be a product of a discrete structure of spacetime itself (as in loop quantum gravity)?
  * Are there deviations from the predictions of general relativity at very small or very large scales or in other extreme circumstances that flow from a quantum gravity theory?


The theory of LQG is one possible solution to the problem of quantum gravity, as is [string theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/String_theory "String theory"). There are substantial differences however. For example, string theory also addresses [unification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unified_field_theory "Unified field theory"), the understanding of all known forces and particles as manifestations of a single entity, by postulating extra dimensions and so-far unobserved additional particles and symmetries. Contrary to this, LQG is based only on quantum theory and general relativity and its scope is limited to understanding the quantum aspects of the gravitational interaction. On the other hand, the consequences of LQG are radical, because they fundamentally change the nature of space and time and provide a tentative but detailed physical and mathematical picture of quantum spacetime. 
Presently, no semiclassical limit recovering general relativity has been shown to exist. This means it remains unproven that LQG's description of spacetime at the [Planck scale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planck_scale "Planck scale") has the right [continuum limit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continuum_limit "Continuum limit") (described by general relativity with possible quantum corrections). Specifically, the dynamics of the theory are encoded in the [Hamiltonian constraint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamiltonian_constraint_of_LQG "Hamiltonian constraint of LQG"), but there is no candidate [Hamiltonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamiltonian_\(quantum_mechanics\) "Hamiltonian \(quantum mechanics\)").[[92]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTENicolaiPeetersZamaklar2005R193%E2%80%93R247-92) Other technical problems include finding [off-shell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Off-shell "Off-shell") closure of the constraint algebra and physical inner product [vector space](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vector_space "Vector space"), coupling to matter fields of [quantum field theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quantum_field_theory "Quantum field theory"), fate of the [renormalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renormalization "Renormalization") of the [graviton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graviton "Graviton") in [perturbation theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perturbation_theory "Perturbation theory") that lead to [ultraviolet divergence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultraviolet_divergence "Ultraviolet divergence") beyond 2-loops (see [one-loop Feynman diagram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One-loop_Feynman_diagram "One-loop Feynman diagram") in [Feynman diagram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feynman_diagram "Feynman diagram")).[[92]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTENicolaiPeetersZamaklar2005R193%E2%80%93R247-92)
While there has been a proposal relating to observation of [naked singularities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naked_singularity "Naked singularity"),[[93]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_gravity#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoswamiJoshiSingh200631302-93) and [doubly special relativity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doubly_special_relativity "Doubly special relativity") as a part of a program called [loop quantum cosmology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_quantum_cosmology "Loop quantum cosmology"), there is no experimental observation for which loop quantum gravity makes a prediction not made by the Standard Model or general relativity (a problem that plagues all current theories of quantum gravity). Because of the above-mentioned lack of a semiclassical limit, LQG has not yet even reproduced the predictions made by general relativity. 
An alternative criticism is that general relativity may be an [effective field theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Effective_field_theory "Effective field theory"), and therefore quantization ignores the fundamental degrees of freedom. 
  

## See also
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=36 "Edit section: See also")]
  * [Problem of time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Problem_of_time "Problem of time") – Conceptual conflict between general relativity and quantum mechanics
  * [Ashtekar variables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashtekar_variables "Ashtekar variables") – Variables used in general relativity
  * [C*-algebra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C*-algebra "C*-algebra") – Topological complex vector space
  * [Category theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category_theory "Category theory") – General theory of mathematical structures
  * [Doubly special relativity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doubly_special_relativity "Doubly special relativity") – Generalization of special relativity
  * [Gelfand–Naimark–Segal construction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gelfand%E2%80%93Naimark%E2%80%93Segal_construction "Gelfand–Naimark–Segal construction") – Correspondence in functional analysis
  * [Group field theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_field_theory "Group field theory") – Quantum field theory with a Lie group base manifold
  * [Heyting algebra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heyting_algebra "Heyting algebra") – Algebraic structure used in logic
  * [Hamiltonian constraint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamiltonian_constraint "Hamiltonian constraint") – Key constraint in some theories admitting Hamiltonian formulations
  * [Hamiltonian constraint of LQG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamiltonian_constraint_of_LQG "Hamiltonian constraint of LQG") – Constraint in loop quantum gravity
  * [Immirzi parameter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immirzi_parameter "Immirzi parameter") – Numerical coefficient in loop quantum gravity
  * [Knot invariant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knot_invariant "Knot invariant") – Function of a knot that takes the same value for equivalent knots
  * [Kodama state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kodama_state "Kodama state") – Zero energy solution to the Schrödinger equation in loop quantum gravity
  * [Lorentz invariance in loop quantum gravity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorentz_invariance_in_loop_quantum_gravity "Lorentz invariance in loop quantum gravity") – Aspect of loop quantum gravity
  * [Noncommutative geometry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noncommutative_geometry "Noncommutative geometry") – Branch of mathematics
  * [Regge calculus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regge_calculus "Regge calculus") – Formalism in general relativity
  * [S-knot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-knot "S-knot") – Equivalence class of spin networks
  * [Spin foam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spin_foam "Spin foam") – Topological structure in loop quantum gravity
  * [String-net liquid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/String-net_liquid "String-net liquid") – Condensed matter physics model involving only closed loops
  * [String theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/String_theory "String theory") – Theory of subatomic structure
  * [Supersymmetry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supersymmetry "Supersymmetry") – Symmetry between bosons and fermions
  * [Topos theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topos "Topos") – Mathematical category
  * [Einstein–Cartan theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Einstein%E2%80%93Cartan_theory "Einstein–Cartan theory") – Classical theory of gravitation

## Further reading
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Loop_quantum_gravity&action=edit&section=40 "Edit section: Further reading")]
  * Popular books: 
    * [Rodolfo Gambini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodolfo_Gambini "Rodolfo Gambini") and [Jorge Pullin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Pullin "Jorge Pullin"), [_Loop Quantum Gravity for Everyone_](https://doi.org/10.1142/11599), [World Scientific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Scientific "World Scientific"), 2020.
    * [Carlo Rovelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Rovelli "Carlo Rovelli"), "Reality is not what it seems", Penguin, 2016.
    * [Martin Bojowald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Bojowald "Martin Bojowald"), _Once Before Time: A Whole Story of the Universe_ 2010.
    * [Carlo Rovelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Rovelli "Carlo Rovelli"), _What is Time? What is space?_ , Di Renzo Editore, Roma, 2006.
    * [Lee Smolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Smolin "Lee Smolin"), _[Three Roads to Quantum Gravity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Roads_to_Quantum_Gravity "Three Roads to Quantum Gravity")_ , 2001.
  * Magazine articles: 
    * [Lee Smolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Smolin "Lee Smolin"), "Atoms of Space and Time", _[Scientific American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_American "Scientific American")_ , January 2004.
    * [Martin Bojowald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Bojowald "Martin Bojowald"), "Following the Bouncing Universe", _[Scientific American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_American "Scientific American")_ , October 2008.
  * Easier introductory, expository or critical works: 
    * [Abhay Ashtekar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abhay_Ashtekar "Abhay Ashtekar"), _Gravity and the quantum_ , e-print available as [gr-qc/0410054](https://arxiv.org/abs/gr-qc/0410054) (2004).
    * [John C. Baez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_C._Baez "John C. Baez") and Javier P. Muniain, _Gauge Fields, Knots and Quantum Gravity_ , World Scientific (1994).
    * [Carlo Rovelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Rovelli "Carlo Rovelli"), _A Dialog on Quantum Gravity_ , e-print available as [hep-th/0310077](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0310077) (2003).
